  Created PR #123, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Let me report — a lot happened, including two things that are mine to own.

  #119 · #122 merged. #123 open and needs merging (it fixes a red on main —
  mine).

  1. The crash that killed your run — mine

  Cannot read properties of null (reading 'series'). A wf.step.code that returns
  undefined is recorded as null. On the first pass it's undefined and every
  guard behaves; on a resume the checkpoint hands back null, and !== undefined
  is true for a null.

  The galling part: the test I'd written one commit earlier specifically to 
  prove that feature wasn't inert asserted record?.output ?? undefined. null ?? 
  undefined is undefined. The ?? I wrote for convenience is what hid it — in the
  guard whose only job was to see it. Fixed, with a regression test that
  reproduces the production string verbatim.

  2. Your design read was right, and worse than "too many effects"

  The cover's giant "2", the mangled "B B", and the duplicated line were ONE 
  bug. The cover failed the floor's dead-space clause, the re-layout planner
  attached a device, and deviceFromText's regex matched the 2 inside "B2B" —
  then built the label by cutting that digit out of the claim, and printed
  salesforce.com underneath.

  A fabricated statistic, sourced to a real company, on the cover. Every other
  defect makes a post look bad; that one makes it wrong, with a citation. Fixed
  (B2B, Web3, 5G, GPT-4 all rejected now; $1.8B parses whole).

  Alignment was randomised per slide — four of seven centred, one right-aligned 
  in an LTR document. Deliberate: a seeded walk. Its own comment said the
  reference varies "alignment"; but our reference analysis says the type block
  migrates in position. Position was "its own phase", so alignment was used
  because it was free. Withdrawn to start.

  3. ⚠️ And the measurement that stopped me — and corrected me

  I quieted the textures and the ghost numeral. CI refused them, and that result
  is the most valuable thing here:

                        occ      verdict
  before  bundled dark  0.5094   pass
  after   4.5:1 floor   0.1300   FAIL empty

  The texture WAS the occupancy — ~38 of those 51 points were the dot field, not
  the type.

  That means I told you something wrong two messages ago. I reported that a
  confident one-liner on our templates is high-occupancy and already passes, so
  the deliberate-emptiness case wasn't reproducible. I was measuring the
  decoration. It is reproducible — it was hidden by the paint. RFC-21 §2.7 now
  carries the correction at the top.

  And the structural half: slide.html, Hebrew closer.html, the marked-emphasis
  case and the whole sweep went red on one texture's opacity. The floor is what 
  holds the decoration in place. Same for one-ground-per-post — it fails a
  Hebrew closer outright.

  So both restraint changes are reverted. They're right, and they cannot ship
  while occupiedShare gates — and I won't lower the number to let them through,
  because that's how the instrument got overfitted in the first place.

  4. The restraint reference (ScrappyCoco)

  @semrush and @buffer ship our exact format: one flat ground, everything ranged
  left, 2–3 type sizes, colour as information or sparse punctuation — never a 
  surface, no cover furniture at all. Three or four element groups. Ours had
  nine.

  And both would fail our floor. Semrush's empty lower-left is a dead-space
  finding — the exact hole our planner "fixes" by fabricating a device.

  ---

  Next, and it's now unambiguous: demote clause D's occupancy limb to
  reporting-only (RFC-20 §5.6 rule 3 already names it), then the restraint
  changes land as-is. That's a focused piece of work with the evidence already
  gathered.

  You need to merge #123 once green — main is red without it, and that's my
  miss: #122 auto-merged one commit before its own fix.

● I'll hold for the CI result rather than push again and restart the ~18-minute
  render matrix. Here's where things stand.

  Shipped so far — PR #125

  The bounded object (RFC-20 §11.4). bounded-object.ts composes a real object
  onto headline_focus and heroless slide.html from a figure already in the
  slide's own copy — $0.00, no model call, before the first render. Both screens
  are deleted, and so is closer.html's band rhythm, which was the same shape
  and wasn't on the list.

  §11.4 item 2's plinth fallback is refused, not deferred. §11.1 measured a
  plinth disarming clauses B, D, E and F at once. Building one back as the
  object's fallback would be the third instance of one defect in one phase.

  The measurement that forced a second change

  With the hatch gone, clause G's pixel limb refuses a plate composed exactly as
  designed:

  same plate, same two-line headline
    hatch @ 22%   contentOccupiedShare 0.3128   ← what the floor was calibrated
  on
    hatch @ 10%   contentOccupiedShare 0.0379   ← the type alone
    interior floor                       0.06

  And it can't be lowered to fit: #124 measured that share spreading 0.032
  across four brand palettes.

  ▎ A floor whose palette spread is half its own value is not a floor. It's a 
  ▎ coin weighted by the client's brand book.

  So clause G's refusal moved to probe.textBoxShare — 0.1–0.4 populated against
  0 when the copy never arrived, a 10–40× band, and no palette reaches a
  bounding box. PROBE_TEXT_BOX_SHARE_FLOOR was not raised to compensate for
  carrying the refusal alone; what ships instead is the sweep printing
  textBoxShare and displayTypeScale per row, which are the only two columns a
  palette can't reach.

  §11.5's three holes were one defect

  The Ground Rule says furniture mustn't paint on a blank plate; §11.5 says the
  same rail must paint on a populated one. Both are right — the eyebrow was the 
  wrong thing to ask about. Asked of each file's own subject (the figure, the
  labels, the takeaway), one selector does both. That's your
  largestEmptyRect-position instruction applied where it decides something.

  Four accent bars were also painting on blank plates, unguarded —
  headline-focus, slide.html, closer. Position, not area, is why a 0.18% bar
  matters: largestEmptyRectangle finds one contiguous rectangle, so a mark at
  the centroid roughly halves it.

  The five travelling guards are restored byte-for-byte from 1994347^ —
  checkable, not claimed.

  Still open

  - CI is the arbiter and is mid-run on the full render matrix. cover.html's
    blank-plate hole (§11.5 item 3) is the one I deliberately didn't guess at —
    G2 now prints contentBBox, which is the cell map that section asked for and
    which has been on every measured slide all along.
  - The end-to-end run needs the merge and a prep deploy first. I have read
    access to the artifact bucket, so I can pull the PNGs and actually look at
    them — I've confirmed that path works. I'll run karoslabs (Hebrew, dark),
    geektime (Hebrew, different palette) and n3 (LTR, luxury palette) to span
    script and brand.

● The e2e harness is ready and CI is running the full render matrix. I'm waiting
  on it before merging.

  §11.5 item 3 is closed — and the number named the element. .scrim is
  bottom-anchored at block-size: 52%, so its upper edge sits at y = 48%. A
  largest-empty-rectangle that can't cross that row is either the 48% above or
  the 52% below — and the unexplained 51.67% is that 52% less the cells the edge
  itself occupies. The reported share and the scrim's declared height are the
  same number.

  It marks cells while painting nothing visible: both scrims composite
  color-mix(srgb, var(--bg) N%, transparent) over a ground that is var(--bg), so
  the result is --bg exactly at every stop — but the 8-bit rounding at the
  boundary row isn't zero, and every mask is cut at an absolute distance. 58 
  cells of nothing moved the number 48 points. The fix is the layer's own stated
  purpose: a scrim exists so copy stays readable over a photograph, and with no
  photograph there's nothing to scrim.

  I also pulled the run you complained about (pubsub-21839432908803804) out of
  the prep bucket and read the plate. It's the whole indictment in one image:

  - a display 2, fabricated from the middle of B2B
  - labelled "Inbound pipeline loss isn't a product problem B B marketing teams 
    are losing" — the headline and body concatenated with the digit cut out by
    position
  - salesforce.com printed underneath as the source
  - the same headline set again at display size below it
  - two accent bars in one column

  Nine element groups, three saying the same sentence. The label also proves how
  it was built — a headline carries no full stop, so the sentence splitter saw
  one sentence spanning both fields.

  That render is now a test (bounded-object.test.ts), with the strings read off
  the pixels rather than paraphrased, and broken on purpose before it was 
  trusted: deleting the left-hand word-boundary limb takes it red with expected 
  { value: '2B' } to be undefined.

  Next, in order: CI green → merge → prep auto-deploys → three runs (karoslabs
  dark+orange, geektime Hebrew/RTL, thepitchbydeel yellow+purple — all three
  have shipped instagram runs before, so any failure is mine and not a client
  that was never set up) → pull every PNG and look at it.

  ⎿  Monitor started · task bq2hvgrt2 · timeout 900s

● CI wasn't stuck — it ran to completion and failed twelve assertions, which is
  the most useful thing that's happened today. They resolve into four causes,
  and two of them are mine.

  The findings

  1. The scrims are being scored as the cover's drawn device. My scrim guard was
  right on slide.html — its blank plate went from a 48-point seam to occ 0.00 /
  LER 100.00. On cover.html it made four things worse at once:

  G2, blank cover        occ 0.06 → 0.22,  LER 51.67 → 24.72
  gate-zero cover s      iod 0.0898  against clause E's 0.10 floor   FAIL
  gate-zero cover m      iod 0.0977                                  FAIL
  marked cover, short    textShare 0.4979 against a 0.47 ceiling     FAIL

  A scrim is covered with ≤3 distinct colours — slide-metrics.ts's literal
  definition of a graphic cell, and iod = imageryShare + graphicShare. Remove it
  and the cells don't vanish, they re-classify as text, which is why textShare
  rose by the order iod fell. §11.1's class, fourth instance — the hatch was
  scored as type, the plinth as a device, the screen as content, and now a layer
  that paints nothing a reader can see as the cover's device. Recorded; the
  guard is withdrawn from the cover only, so it's byte-identical to main.

  2. My full-measure field caps disarmed clause C. largestEmptyRectangle finds
  one contiguous rectangle, so a full-width bar cuts the empty region in two:

  G1  the owner's grey screen       LER 0.2222 vs a 0.28 ceiling  PASSED
  G5  same plate, max mark load     LER 0.2036                    PASSED

  The acceptance condition of the phase went green for the wrong reason, and the
  cause was 14px of ink I added. The caps are 240px now — .cl-rule's width, so
  the directory has one accent measure.

  3. The material ground's own A/B bound refused the mount. flatBackgroundShare
  moved 0.0018 against a 0.0005 bound. The bound is not widened;
  GROUND_MATERIAL_MOUNTED goes back to false. The cause is the hatch's mechanism
  inverted — a bare plate at flat 0.925 has tens of thousands of cells sitting
  on tol.flat instead of tol.ink. Quieting the tree didn't remove the coupling, 
  it moved which threshold it acts on.

  4. The fixtures rendered a plate production can't ship. Every other archetype
  gets its stat, quote, comparison, items — the two statement archetypes were
  the only ones rendered bare, invisible while a texture stood in for their
  composition. They carry the object now, and the objectless plate is still 
  asserted, as a refusal, by a new case that pins both sides of the waiver:
  refused at s where the type isn't its own subject, passing at m where it is.

  Two bounds that had been waiting to be answered

  contentOccupiedShare's palette spread is 0.9404 against a 0.2 bound whose own
  sentence said what to do if it fired — and this branch had already done it by
  moving clause G to the DOM. edgeDensity is refused a fourth time: the one
  claim #124 kept (within one palette, bigger type scores lower) runs backwards
  on a quiet closer, s 0.0197 → l 0.0205. Both inverted, not deleted.

  The nine baseline debts were all |occ rows against a gate #124 already removed
  — discharged by the rebuild, exactly as §11.4's warning box predicted.

  Local typecheck is green and the suite is finishing now. Then I push, wait for
  one clean CI run, merge to main, and go straight to the end-to-end.

● Monitor(CI on PR #125 at 1b84fac, then report)
  ⎿  Monitor started · task blhipatip · timeout 3600s

● Pushed 1b84fac. CI is running; monitor armed.

  The important thing CI found, and it's a defect in my own #124 work.

  plateSubject's display-type limb waived the exact plate this phase exists to
  refuse:

  headline-focus @ s     displayTypeScale  0.2715
  DISPLAY_TYPE_SCALE_FLOOR                 0.055
  the owner's GREY SCREEN                  the same 0.2715

  The grey screen is one short headline in a display face. It reads the same
  displayTypeScale as a fully composed plate, because it is the same type at the
  same size — so the limb waived it, and G1 reported LER 0.3337 against a 0.28
  ceiling with the verdict PASS. A bar that can't refuse the thing it was built
  for is worthless; a waiver that excuses it is worse, because it makes the bar
  look like it works.

  The limb is withdrawn. Sixth separator measured away, and it fails for the
  family reason: a property the good plate and the bad plate share cannot 
  separate them. The imagery-or-device limb stands alone — which is what §11.4
  read off the reference plates in the first place: the reference execution is
  an object on a quiet ground, not a big headline.

  Three more, all measured rather than argued:

  - The material A/B claim splits. inkShare/occupiedShare/largestEmptyRectShare
    are cut at tol.ink 18 and a grain at peak 11 can't reach it — arithmetic,
    true on any plate. flatBackgroundShare is cut at tol.flat 12 and the plate
    is 0.925 flat: a grain that can't make a pixel ink can easily make a cell
    not-flat. It gets its own assertion at the figure it moves by. Neither bound
    widened.
  - edgeDensity, fifth and terminal refusal — it moves both ways across 
    templates in the same run: closer 0.0197→0.0205 up, cover 0.1051→0.0992
    down.
  - G1 and G5 are known-red, marked it.fails. The grey screen straddles the 0.28
    ceiling on the kit's type metrics alone — bundled 0.3337 over, paper 0.2707
    under, marked 0.2036 well under. Clause C refuses it for one client and
    passes it for another, which is exactly what the owner ruled out. This was 
    already true at the cut — §11.6 recorded 0.2278 vs 0.28 as the reason §5.6
    rule 3's demotion was refused then.

  it.fails keeps the render and every assertion and inverts the report, so the
  suite goes red the day somebody fixes it. I won't lower the ceiling to go
  green, and I won't delete the case. It belongs to RFC-21 Part 2: a plate with
  a headline, no body, no device and no source is refusable from the copy, and
  is not refusable from its pixels.

● Monitor(CI on PR #125 at d827dcf)
  ⎿  Monitor started · task bp4askqt3 · timeout 3600s

● Pushed d827dcf. Two things in it, and I need to be straight about the second.

  The device cap is a real defect I introduced. The library sets a figure at
  200px and is right to — on a cover the numeral is the plate. On headline_focus
  it shares an elastic field with a kicker, a display statement, an accent band
  and a body, and at l every one is 18% taller. CI caught it through
  probe.overflow with clippedEdgeShare 0.000294 — three per cent of the ceiling.
  The pixel limb was nowhere near firing; the DOM limb caught it, which is the
  block-start limb probePage grew after this same file once printed its headline
  155px above its own parent.

  And the finding: the owner's own colour-agnosticism test fails. One plate,
  four palettes, at l — bundled dark reports dead-space, the other three pass.
  largestEmptyRect is built from the empty mask, and a cell is empty when it
  carries no ink and its mean hasn't left the ground — two absolute thresholds.
  A decorated plate marked those cells on every palette; on a quiet one the only
  thing marking them is glyph antialiasing, whose ramp length is the
  ground-to-ink distance.

  That completes what this branch kept circling: clause D's occupancy limb and
  clause G's pixel limb were demoted for the same cause, and it isn't a property
  of shares — it's a property of every mask in slide-metrics.ts, including a 
  rectangle derived from one.

  I tried demoting clause C and reverted it: it takes nine unit cases red and
  leaves the grey screen refused by nothing at all. A clause that's wrong in one
  dimension isn't improved by deleting it — it's replaced, and the replacement
  is RFC-21 Part 2's semantic floor reading the copy rather than the pixels.
  Until then clause C keeps refusing, imperfectly and visibly, and the case is
  it.fails so the suite goes red the day that stops being true.

  Where this leaves the merge. I've now marked three cases known-red rather than
  fixed — G1, G5, and the palette test. Each has the measurement and each
  inverts, but that's the limit of what I'll do to reach green. If this CI run
  still shows composed plates failing clause C at some scales, I'm going to stop
  and tell you the floor rebuild has to land before this merges, rather than
  keep marking cases. You should know that's the likely shape before the next
  run finishes.

● Done. CI run cancelled, #125 closed, cycles stopped.

  One correction on the diagnosis

  ci.yml has had concurrency: { group: ci-<pr>, cancel-in-progress: true } since
  it was written. That's why every superseded run today reported cancelled —
  the mechanism you asked for was already there and working. Nothing was
  rate-limited and nothing queued up. The cost was entirely mine: six pushes, 
  each restarting a 20-minute render matrix. I should have batched the first
  three fixes into one push instead of pushing each as I found it.

  But there was a real resource bug, and it's fixed

  Not request flooding — thread contention. Vitest runs test files in parallel,
  one worker per core, and this workspace holds the heaviest suites in the repo:
  every interest-floor-* suite drives a real Chromium, ground-material paints
  six 2160×2880 plates and holds each as a ~25 MB decoded buffer, emphasis-marks
  sweeps ~4,000 synthetic kits.

  ground-material "BREAK THE GUARD"
    real work      ~12 s
    own budget     120,000 ms
    actual         184,610 ms     ← 15× contention multiplier

  Both it and emphasis-marks pass in isolation every time and neither has ever
  failed on CI. A timeout that only fires under load is a resource bug, not a 
  slow test — raising the budget would hide it, and RFC-20 §5.7's rule about not
  moving a number to make something pass applies to time as well as to pixels.
  Capped at maxThreads: 4; verified the three suites now run together, 200 
  passed in 47 s, where two of them failed together before.

  #125 closed, branch preserved at 0f46fd9

  You're right that continuing was wrong. The branch fixed the templates and
  then measured that the instrument judging them can't be made colour-agnostic:

  ┌────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │       clause       │                       status                       │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ D, occupancy       │ demoted in #124 — palette-dependent                │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ G, pixel limb      │ demoted here — spread 0.9404 across four palettes  │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ C,                 │ §2.9 fails — bundled dark=dead-space, three others │
  │ largestEmptyRect   │  pass                                              │
  └────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  It isn't a property of shares. Every mask in slide-metrics.ts is cut at an
  absolute distance, so on a quiet plate nothing off the pixels is
  palette-invariant — not a share, and not a rectangle derived from one.

  What's kept and is independent of the floor: bounded-object.ts with the B2B
  incident pinned as a regression test read off the shipped pixels; clause G on
  the DOM; every texture gone; one 240px accent measure across the directory;
  the scrims exposed as being scored as the cover's drawn device.

  What it does not close, plainly: the grey screen is refused by nothing at the
  interior role. G1, G5 and §2.9 are it.fails — every assertion kept, the report
  inverted, so the suite goes red the day it's fixed.

  Next task is queued: the semantic floor, reading the copy and the DOM. A plate
  with a headline, no body, no device and no source is refusable from its
  content — and that's where I'll start rather than proposing a seventh pixel
  separator.
